import { useEffect, useRef } from "react";
import type { SectionPhase } from "./section";

/**
 * One quiet element that moves with her voice, and the only evidence on screen
 * that the microphone is alive.
 *
 * For twenty-one minutes she talks into a blank card. The recorder already
 * reports a REFUSED microphone, but it cannot see a live-but-silent one -- a
 * muted input, a device the OS switched underneath her, a headset that dropped
 * off. That failure produces a perfect-looking run, a clean upload, and a
 * Report Card of zeros, and she finds out at the end of the twenty-one minutes.
 *
 * So this does two jobs with one reading.
 *
 * **Presence.** A bar that answers "is anything reaching it" without answering
 * "how am I doing". Deliberately not a level meter with a target: nothing here
 * may look like a score, because nothing is judged until the section ends.
 *
 * **The fault.** If the input never leaves the noise floor across a whole
 * stimulus, that is said out loud at the boundary, while there is still a
 * section left to save -- and unsaid the moment anything is heard again.
 *
 * Nothing here re-renders the runner. The bar is written to as a DOM node
 * inside an animation frame, because a component that set state at 60Hz would
 * be re-rendering the drill room underneath her while she reads.
 */

/**
 * The line between "an open microphone in a quiet room" and "nothing arriving".
 *
 * RMS over the raw time-domain samples, so it is amplitude and not loudness. A
 * genuinely dead input sits at or near zero; a live one in a silent room still
 * carries the room. Set low on purpose: this exists to catch nothing at all,
 * never to judge a quiet speaker, and a false "your microphone is dead" during
 * a real run is worse than the fault it reports.
 */
const NOISE_FLOOR = 0.004;

/**
 * How long a phase has to run before its silence means anything.
 *
 * A Recall she ends in three seconds carries no evidence either way, and
 * accusing the hardware on that is how a warning stops being believed. A window
 * shorter than this counts neither for the fault nor against it.
 */
const MIN_WINDOW_MS = 8000;

/**
 * Consecutive silent windows before the fault is raised.
 *
 * Both halves of a stimulus are spoken -- "Read it. Tell it back and colour it in
 * as you go", then "Say the whole argument back from memory" -- so a dead input
 * is silent through every window there is. A skip is silent through exactly
 * one: she had nothing for that Recall, and then she reads the next stimulus
 * aloud like all the others.
 *
 * One window was therefore the wrong unit, and it accused the hardware on the
 * most ordinary thing a student does. Two consecutive is the shortest run a skip
 * cannot produce, and it costs a genuinely dead microphone one further stimulus
 * before it is named.
 */
const SILENT_WINDOWS = 2;

/** Full deflection. Chosen so ordinary speech uses most of the bar. */
const FULL_SCALE = 0.15;

export interface VoiceLevelProps {

   * told a student her hardware was broken, and went on telling her for the
   * remaining twenty-five stimuli and four minutes of clearly heard speech.
   */
  onDeadInput?: (dead: boolean) => void;
}

/* Presence, not a level meter.
 *
 * The one moving thing on a screen she talks at for twenty-one minutes. It
 * answers "is anything reaching it" and deliberately not "how am I doing":
 * no target, no colour change, no number, because nothing in this room is
 * allowed to read as a verdict before the section ends. Graphite, low
 * contrast, and beside the instruction rather than anywhere near the text.
 */
.ac-section-level {
  display: inline-block;
  width: 34px;
  height: 3px;
  margin-left: 0.625rem;
  overflow: hidden;
  border-radius: 9999px;
  background: #e2e8f0;
  vertical-align: middle;
}

.ac-section-level-fill {
  display: block;
  height: 100%;
  transform: scaleX(0);
  transform-origin: left center;
  background: var(--sec-chrome);
}

// usage: node rep.mjs <file> <<< JSON [{from,to}] ; exact, each must match once
import fs from "node:fs";
const [file] = process.argv.slice(2);
const edits = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(0, "utf8"));

for (const { from, to } of edits) {

/**
 * A preview harness for the section launch surface. Development only.
 *
 * It exists so the component can be looked at in a real browser, in every state
 * and at every breakpoint, without signing in and without a graded section
 * sitting in the database. Only the network is stubbed: `window.fetch` is
 * replaced before anything imports the Supabase client, so the component, the
 * client helper and the derivation all run exactly as they do in the product.
 *
 * Not routed and not imported by the app. Reached at
 * `/src/dev/section-launch-preview.html` on the dev server. It is not an entry
 * point in the Vite build, which only builds `index.html`, so it never ships.
 *
 * States: ?state=runs|oneRun|empty|failed|noHistory|library  ·  ?theme=light|dark
 */
import { createRoot } from "react-dom/client";
// The section JSONs deliberately ship an empty instructions block -- the rules
// are authored once, in one file, and copied in at seed time -- so the harness
// reads the same instructions file the migration seeds.

import type { ActivityInstructions } from "../features/activity-centre/api/types";

const params = new URLSearchParams(location.search);
const state = params.get("state") ?? "runs";
const theme = params.get("theme") ?? "light";

function stim(item_order: number, over: Record<string, unknown> = {}) {
  return {
    slug: `s${item_order}`,
    item_order,
    carried: 3,
    total: 3,
    percent: 100,
    band: "at-the-standard",
    missed: [],
    decoysCarried: [],
    unjudged: [],
    reciting: false,
    listing: false,
    coloured: true,
    colourSpecific: true,
    dead_air_seconds: 0,
    spoken: "she said something here",
    ...over,
  };
}

const SLOTS = 25;
function run(seconds: number, createdAt: string, gaps: number[] = [], silent: number[] = []) {
  return {
    assignment_id: `assign-${seconds}`,
    attempt_no: 1,
    source_section: "PT105.S2",
    graded_at: createdAt,
    created_at: createdAt,
    stimuli: Array.from({ length: SLOTS }, (_, i) => {
      const n = i + 1;
      if (silent.includes(n)) return stim(n, { spoken: "", carried: 0, percent: 0 });
      if (gaps.includes(n)) return stim(n, { ungraded: "judge returned nothing" });
      return stim(n);

/**
 * A preview harness for the runner's ready beat. Development only.
 *
 * `SectionStage` is presentational, so the beat can be looked at in every one
 * of its states without a microphone, a signed-in student or a section in the
 * database. Nothing is stubbed: `getStream` returns null, so the bar mounts
 * and stays flat, which is what a refused or silent input looks like too.
 *
 * Not routed and not imported by the app. Reached at
 * `/src/dev/section-beat-preview.html` on the dev server. Not an entry point
 * in the Vite build, which only builds `index.html`, so it never ships.
 *
 * States: ?state=holding|heard|unheard|refused|running
 */
import { createRoot } from "react-dom/client";
import { SectionStage } from "../features/activity-centre/recall/SectionStage";
import type { SectionStimulus } from "../features/activity-centre/recall/section";
import "../features/activity-centre/recall/section.css";

const state = new URLSearchParams(location.search).get("state") ?? "holding";

const stimulus = {
  slug: "pt-105-s2-q01",
  chunks: [
    { n: 1, text: "The council's own survey contradicts the figure it published." },
    { n: 2, text: "So the figure cannot be the one the report relied on." },
  ],
} as SectionStimulus;

const beat = state !== "running";

createRoot(document.getElementById("root")!).render(
  <SectionStage
    stimulus={stimulus}
    phase="working"
    stimulusNumber={1}
    totalStimuli={26}
    error={null}
    elapsedSeconds={0}
    goalSeconds={1500}
    sectionName="PT105.S2"
    recording={state !== "refused"}
    getStream={() => null}
    countdown={beat ? 3 : null}
    micRefused={state === "refused"}
    micHeard={state === "heard"}
    micChecked={state === "heard" || state === "unheard"}
    onExit={() => {}}
    onRetryMic={() => {}}
  />,
);
EOF

command: cd /c/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os && node - <<'EOF'
const fs=require("fs");const p="wiki/RRC-DRILL-AUDIT-CRITIQUE-2026-09-09.md";let s=fs.readFileSync(p,"utf8");
const old='Next: Step 8, the ready beat. "Microphone on. Starting in 3." with the level bar, the sentence moved to the briefing foot, and the beat held until the level has moved once or six seconds have passed, so "check your microphone" is a real instruction rather than a three-second wish.';
if(!s.includes(old)){console.error("anchor missing");process.exit(1)}
const add=`Next: Step 8, the ready beat.

## Step 8: done, 2026-09-09

Branch \`rrc-step-8\`. The beat is now the one thing only the beat can do.

- **"Microphone on." and the bar, nothing else.** The thirty-word instruction ("One take, all the way through, out loud. Space twice per stimulus...") left the beat and sits at the foot of the briefing, where she has as long as she likes. The foot also stops saying the timer starts the moment she presses Start, which was no longer true once the beat existed.
- **The check is real.** \`VoiceLevel\` gains \`onHeard\`, fired once on the first reading above the noise floor. The runner holds the count until that fires or \`MIC_CHECK_SECONDS\` (6) runs out, whichever is first, and the check's clock starts when the microphone opens rather than at mount, so a slow permission prompt cannot eat it. A zero-second beat has no check, which is what every existing test relies on.
- **Three states, three sentences.** Holding: "Say something to check your microphone." with no count showing, because a number ticking beside "say something" says the opposite. Heard: "Heard you." and "Starting in 3". Expired unheard: "Nothing heard yet. Check it is not muted." and the count. The footer status announces the one transition that matters ("Heard you. The section starts after the count.").
- **One bar at a time.** During the beat the bar renders in the panel at 160x4, where she is looking; once running it is back on the rail at 34x3. It is the same element and the same ink, and it still never reads as a score.
- Measured in the in-app browser on the new \`section-beat-preview.html\` harness (\`?state=holding|heard|unheard|refused|running\`): panel bar 160x4, no rail bar during the beat, one rail bar once running.
- Tests: three new beat tests (holds unheard, counts once the check runs out, counts as soon as it hears her, the last through a fake \`AudioContext\`). The old "actually counts once the ready beat is over" test opts out of the check, since jsdom has nothing for it to hear through. Suite 106 files / 2787 tests, \`tsc\` clean.

Worth knowing: the "heard" test cannot be written with a polling query. The footer status also says "Heard you", so \`getByText(/heard you/i)\` finds two elements; the exact string "Heard you." finds the panel.

Next: Step 9, the quieter door and its tokens.`;
s=s.replace(old,add);fs.writeFileSync(p,s);console.log("plan updated");
EOF

RRC: the beat waits to hear her before it counts

"Say something to check your microphone" was printed over a three-second
count that ran whether or not anything was ever heard. The beat now holds
until the level has moved once, or six seconds, whichever is first, and
says which of those it is doing. The thirty-word instruction that used to
share those three seconds moves to the foot of the briefing, and the bar
moves into the panel for the beat so it is where she is looking.

Step 8 of the RRC drill fix plan.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF

**Production answer to the open question.** Both Step 6 and Step 7 are live on lsatprep.study. The bundle moved from `index-Bv9106bQ.js` to `index-CEIwSuho.js`, and the chunks carry the Step 6 markers, the Step 7 "Recorded" and "met on" strings, and the handover ground as `#e9e4ed`, which is the hex form the build gives the oklch value. Three markers from the handoff list came back empty only because minification rewrote them.

**What Step 8 changed**, commit `41fc2e0` on `rrc-step-8`:

- The beat now reads "Microphone on." with the level bar in the panel, and nothing else. The thirty-word Space instruction moved to the briefing foot, which also stops claiming the timer starts the moment she presses Start.
- The count does not begin until the microphone has heard something once, or six seconds pass, whichever is first. Holding shows "Say something to check your microphone." with no number. Heard shows "Heard you." and the count. Expired unheard shows "Nothing heard yet. Check it is not muted." and the count.
- The check's clock starts when the microphone opens, not at mount, so a slow permission prompt cannot eat it. A zero-second beat has no check, which keeps every existing test as it was.
- New harness at `src/dev/section-beat-preview.html?state=holding|heard|unheard|refused|running`, used to verify the three states and the bar placement in the browser.

Suite is 106 files, 2787 tests, `tsc` clean. Plan file has a dated "Step 8: done" section.

**One thing worth knowing.** A polling query for "heard you" finds two elements, because the footer status also says it. The test uses the exact panel string.

**Not done.** Vault wrapup is still outstanding across three sessions. The merged `rrc-step-6` and `rrc-step-7` branches are still there. Step 9, the quieter door, is next.

import fs from "node:fs";
import path from "node:path";
import { describe, expect, it } from "vitest";

const CSS = fs.readFileSync(path.join(__dirname, "section-launch.css"), "utf8");

/**
 * A `var(--name)` whose custom property is never defined does not fail loudly.
 * It falls back, or it inherits, and the result is text nobody can read that no
 * test notices. `--rd-ink-soft` did exactly that here: two rules asked for it,
 * it had never been declared, and the section headings quietly rendered fainter
 * than the body copy underneath them.
 *
 * CLAUDE.md carries the same warning from an earlier round, where three token
 * names shipped undefined for five months and removed the focus ring from every
 * shared Button.
 */
describe("section-launch.css", () => {
  const defined = new Set([...CSS.matchAll(/(--rd-[a-z0-9-]+)\s*:/g)].map((m) => m[1]));
  const used = [...CSS.matchAll(/var\((--rd-[a-z0-9-]+)/g)].map((m) => m[1]);

  it("defines every --rd- token it uses", () => {
    const missing = [...new Set(used)].filter((n) => !defined.has(n));
    expect(missing).toEqual([]);
  });

  it("gives the two buttons their own focus ring, not the world's", () => {
    // `.sl :focus-visible` sets one ring for the whole world and loses the
    // cascade on these two classes, so Start and Back matched :focus-visible
    // and computed outline-style: none. Measured in the browser, not inferred.
    expect(CSS).toContain(".sl .sl-start:focus-visible");
    expect(CSS).toContain(".sl .sl-brief:focus-visible");
    // The door draws both on a dark panel, where an ink ring is invisible.
    expect(CSS).toContain(".rd-hero .sl-start:focus-visible");
  });

  it("sways the grass in groups, not once per blade", () => {
    // 170 blades each carrying their own keyframe put 191 animations on the
    // compositor at rest, for a picture that is usually scrolled past.
    expect(CSS).not.toContain(".mw-blades path {");
    expect(CSS).toContain(".mw-blades g {");
    // Skew, not rotation: rotation about a shared origin lifts the far blades
    // off the ground line, and the planted base is the whole look.
    expect(CSS).toMatch(/@keyframes mw-sway\s*\{[^@]*skewX/);
  });

  it("stops every loop when its surface leaves the screen", () => {
    for (const sel of [".mw[data-still]", ".rd-demo[data-still]", ".rd-method[data-still]"]) {
      expect(CSS).toContain(sel);
    }
    expect(CSS).toContain("animation-play-state: paused");
  });

  it("promises the compositor nothing for the layer that never moves", () => {
    const grain = CSS.match(/\.mw-grain\s*\{([^}]*)\}/)?.[1] ?? "";
    expect(grain).toMatch(/will-change:\s*auto/);
  });

  it("gives the demonstration an ink focus ring, not the hero's white one", () => {
    // .rd-hero :focus-visible is white for the dark panel. The demo card is
    // cream paper sitting on it, where white measured 1.05:1.
    expect(CSS).toContain(".rd-hero .rd-demo :focus-visible");
  });

  it("makes the three small controls pressable at 44px", () => {
    // Visual size is unchanged; the target is an overlay, the way the runner's
    // rail controls were done. Measured in the browser: 44x44, 73x47, 58x47.
    expect(CSS).toMatch(/\.rd-demo-play::after\s*\{[^}]*inset:\s*-8px/);
    expect(CSS).toMatch(/\.sl-crumb-back::after\s*\{[^}]*inset:\s*-14px -8px/);
    const tab = CSS.match(/\.sl-dr-tab\s*\{([^}]*)\}/)?.[1] ?? "";
    expect(tab).toMatch(/padding:\s*10px 0 14px/);
  });

  it("keeps the way out above the hero copy", () => {
    // Same layer, and the copy comes later in the DOM, so on a phone it took
    // the bottom half of the crumb's target with it.
    const zIndexOf = (selector: string) => {
      const after = CSS.split(`${selector} {`)[1] ?? "";
      return Number(after.slice(0, after.indexOf("}")).match(/z-index:\s*(\d+)/)?.[1]);
    };
    expect(zIndexOf(".rd-hero-top")).toBeGreaterThan(zIndexOf(".rd-hero-copy"));
  });

  it("draws no text in flat white on the briefing page, wh...

## Step 9: done, 2026-09-09

- **The three hover lifts are gone.** Start, the step cards and the section Go button change colour on hover and nothing else; the transitions list only the property that still moves. The step card's hover is now a 3:1 edge instead of a 4px rise and a 44px shadow.

- **Tokens.** \`--rd-key-edge\` and \`--rd-key-edge-soft\` name the seven \`#cfc9bd\` and three \`#d9d3c6\`. Raw \`#e3ded2\`, \`#2a2450\`, \`#1a1635\` and \`#f3c9a0\` now use the tokens that already existed, and the two \`var(--x, fallback)\` forms lost their fallbacks. 32 rgba literals became 3 (the token definitions).

- Measured in the in-app browser on the launch harness, \`?state=runs\` and \`?state=library\`: close and attempt borders \`rgb(139, 135, 154)\`, flat rows \`rgb(227, 222, 210)\`, transitions \`background 0.2s\` / \`border-color 0.2s\`, no chip, no dots, meadow under \`.sl\`.

Step 9 of the RRC drill fix plan (wiki/RRC-DRILL-AUDIT-CRITIQUE-2026-09-09.md). Branched off `main`, independent of #105.

- Three hover lifts removed (Start, step cards, section Go): colour change only, transitions trimmed to match.

=== noKeyboard
import { useEffect, useState } from "react";

/**
 * Whether this device probably has no keyboard, so the drill's one key is out
 * of reach.
 *
 * A section advances on Space and on nothing else
 * (`SectionRunner.tsx`, "One key, and only Space"). That is deliberate: no
 * mouse in the loop, so her hands never leave the argument. It also means a
 * phone can open the drill, read stimulus 1, and never get to stimulus 2 --
 * the briefing, the door and the library all render fine at 390x844, so
 * nothing warns her before the recording is already running.
 *
 * `(pointer: coarse)` is the test rather than a width, because the question is
 * "is there a keyboard", not "is the screen small". A narrow desktop window has
 * a keyboard and must keep the pure one-key loop; a large tablet has no
 * keyboard and needs the control. Width answers neither.
 *
 * This is a hint, never a gate. Joshua's call, 2026-09-04: a phone is warned
 * and let through, not refused. So nothing here disables anything -- it adds a
 * sentence at the door and an on-screen way to advance inside the run, and
 * Space stays authoritative everywhere.
 */
export const NO_KEYBOARD_QUERY = "(pointer: coarse)";

/** Read once, outside React, so a non-browser render has a defined answer. */
export function hasNoKeyboard(): boolean {
  if (typeof window === "undefined" || typeof window.matchMedia !== "function") return false;
  return window.matchMedia(NO_KEYBOARD_QUERY).matches;
}

/**
 * The same question as a hook, kept live.
 *
 * A tablet with a keyboard case attached or removed mid-run changes the answer,
 * and the control appearing or going away is the correct response to that. The
 * initial state is computed rather than defaulted to `false`, so the first
 * paint on a phone already carries the control instead of flashing a screen
 * that cannot be advanced.
 */
export function useNoKeyboard(): boolean {
  const [noKeyboard, setNoKeyboard] = useState<boolean>(hasNoKeyboard);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (typeof window === "undefined" || typeof window.matchMedia !== "function") return;
    const mql = window.matchMedia(NO_KEYBOARD_QUERY);
    const onChange = () => setNoKeyboard(mql.matches);
    onChange();
    // `addEventListener` is not universal on MediaQueryList in older Safari,
    // which is exactly the population this query is about, so the older API is
    // the fallback rather than the assumption.
    if (typeof mql.addEventListener === "function") {
      mql.addEventListener("change", onChange);
      return () => mql.removeEventListener("change", onChange);
    }
    mql.addListener?.(onChange);
    return () => mql.removeListener?.(onChange);
  }, []);

  return noKeyboard;
}

=== DESIGN 140-180

One institution with two registers, and the seam between them is deliberate.

**The Hall** is the door. Unauthenticated, dark, and material: near-black ground, warm marble text, a bronze that carries the light, and a serif that behaves like carved lettering. Its own source calls it a museum nocturne, carved marble filmed in darkness, read by modern instruments. Its job is to make a prospective student feel they are entering somewhere, not signing up for something. It is the only place in the product where atmosphere outranks throughput.

**The Workroom** is everything past sign-in. Light, neutral, and quiet, because a student sits inside it for an hour reading dense argument text and the interface must not compete with the passage. There is no warmth here and that is the decision, not an oversight: warm accents need a dark ground to read, and on the Workroom's white they collapse. The Hall's bronze measures 8.58:1 on its own night and 2.30:1 on the Workroom's page. The two registers do not share a palette because they cannot.

What they do share is restraint about meaning. In both, colour is a signal. Green, red and amber say something happened. Nothing is coloured to be warm, to look premium, or to fill a space. Depth comes from hairlines and background tone rather than shadow, and hierarchy comes from size, weight and position rather than hue.

Inside the Workroom, individual rooms may commit fully to their own world. The eight Activity Centre worlds, the scoped bootcamps and the inbox each carry a painted identity behind a wrapper class. That is sanctioned and it is what an academy is: neutral corridors, characterful rooms. The corridor never takes on a room's paint.

**Key Characteristics...

=== brief 45-100
## Read, Recall, Colour door (locked 2026-09-05)

The two RRC doors (`SectionLibraryLaunch`, `SectionLaunch`, both drawn by `SectionLaunchFrame`) are a committed world of their own, mode **Persuade** at the top and **Operate** in the slab. Joshua's brief: a layered, high-end landing page in the spirit of heylemon.ai (cream ground, painted panels, serif display with italic emphasis, mono micro-labels, a dictation demo) with fora.so's depth-plane hero, and bold step 1 to 4 instructions.

- **World:** one dusk. The runner's indigo (`#1a1635`) lifted into a sky over a painted meadow, cream paper (`#f6f3ea`) beneath. Display is Fraunces at opsz 144; body Inter; labels IBM Plex Mono. Marigold marks the Colour step, emerald marks a carried detail, nothing else is coloured.
- **Hero:** `MeadowScene` paints five depth planes generatively in SVG (seeded ridges, seeded grass blades, feTurbulence grain) so no raster ships; each plane rides `useScroll` at its own rate and the foreground grass crosses in front of the demo card. The headline blurs and recedes on the same scroll value.
- **Signature moment:** `RecallDemo`, a looping performance of the method on a synthetic stimulus, labelled as such, whose text literally dissolves at the Recall step.
- **Steps:** four cards, each with a small drawn scene, doubling as the ARIA tablist the tests already contract (`sl-beat`, `sl-beat-tab-N`, `sl-beat-panel`). The full briefing is the second tab of the drawer.
- **Tokens:** scoped to `.sl` as `--rd-*`. The design detector reports them as outside DESIGN.md; that is the sanctioned-world exception above, not drift.

## The Report Card (locked 2026-09-08)

Its own world, scoped to `.rc` in `report-card.css`. Joshua's brief: highly
organised, easy to run through, high-end, with her recording and the standard's
side by side and the transcript following the sound. Explicitly **not cream, not
emerald, and not grey** ("the cream paper colour is overdone and too AI
looking", "I don't want emerald", "it should be much more colorful"). The second
sanctioned departure from the wing's surface after the RRC door.

- **Two grounds.** A deep plum console (`oklch(0.245 0.058 312)`) holds the
  verdict, the transport and the ledger; a violet-cast white page
  (`oklch(0.979 0.008 312)`) holds everything she reads at length.
- **Colour means a KIND of feedback, never a severity.** The mechanic is
  Grammarly's, which colours by category and reserves red for outright errors.
  Four fixed tones: **Detail** plum-rose, **Read** teal, **Colour** gold,
  **Method** indigo. A miss is the colour of the thing it is a miss of, which is
  what stops twenty-two stimuli of corrections reading as a heat map of failure.
- **Signal magenta is sound, not a category.** It marks the line currently
  playing and nothing else, held at hue 328 so it separates from the
  plum-rose dropped chips it shares a line with.
- **Hues chosen from research, not taste** (Firecrawl, 2026-09-08). Saturated
  right now: acid yellow, electric cobalt, orange and coral, warm sand and
  terracotta, black plus one neon accent. Under-used: violet and plum, soft
  cyan, dusty rose as a field, complex cool neutrals. Green and cream are the
  owner's veto; orange-red was this file's first answer and is on the over-used
  list. The neutrals are tinted toward violet the way Material 3's baseline is
  (`#fffbfe` paper, `#7d5260` plum tertiary), a system built to hold AA.
- **Band is fill, not hue.** The ledger's marks differ by how full they are, in
  gold; a recital is dashed and a lost one is a hollow rose ring. It survives
  grayscale, which is what lets category own the colour.

export default function ActivityLab() {
  const [world, setWorld] = useState<World>("switchyard");

  return (
    <div className="activity-centre min-h-screen">
      <header className="flex flex-wrap items-center gap-4 overflow-hidden border-b border-border px-6 py-4">
        <div className="mr-auto">
          <p className="text-xs font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.2em] text-muted-foreground">
            Activity Lab · dev only
          </p>
          <h1 className="ac-display text-xl text-foreground">
            {WORLDS.find((w) => w.key === world)!.label}
          </h1>
        </div>
        <Tabs
          options={WORLDS.map((w) => w.key)}
          value={world}
          onChange={(v) => setWorld(v)}
          label="World"
        />
      </header>

      {world === "list" ? <ListPanel /> : null}
      {world === "intro" ? <IntroPanel /> : null}
      {world === "door" ? <DoorPanel /> : null}
      {world === "section" ? <SectionPanel /> : null}
      {world === "report" ? <ReportPanel /> : null}
[/external_agent_tool_result]

{

  "product": "# Product\n\n<!-- impeccable:product-schema 1 -->\n\n## Scope\n\nApp-wide only. Every statement in this file must be true of all of LSAT U.\n\nA rule that binds one surface does not belong here. It goes in that surface's brief under `.impeccable/surfaces/`. This split is deliberate and is the correction to a real failure: the previous PRODUCT.md was written on 2026-05-02 for the Intro to LR bootcamp alone, then widened app-wide on 2026-05-08 in a commit whose stated purpose was adding messenger principles. Bootcamp anti-references silently became binding on the drills, and stayed binding for four months. A one-surface rule written into an app-wide file has no boundary that can hold it. Keeping the file app-wide only removes the mechanism.\n\nThe repo already carries eight bounded contexts with their own glossaries (`CONTEXT-MAP.md`). Surface-specific product truth follows those boundaries, not this file.\n\n## Platform\n\nweb\n\n## Users\n\n**Primary: Aspiring Attorneys clients.** Students who are paying Joshua for one-to-one LSAT coaching. They have consultations, scheduled sessions, transcripts, assigned work, and a real back-and-forth with him. The platform is where their coaching lives between sessions. They arrive already knowing who their coach is, and design decisions are made for them first.\n\n**Secondary: independent self-study learners.** Students using the platform without an active coaching relationship. They are served by the same surfaces but are not the audience a tradeoff is resolved in favour of.\n\n**Operator: Joshua.** The sole instructor and the product, voice, and content authority. He assigns work, publishes material, reviews student activity, and answers messages. He works from any device at any hour, so operator surfaces are as responsive as student surfaces.\n\n## Product Purpose\n\nLSAT U is the operational platform for Joshua's coaching practice. It delivers the method, holds the student's work, and keeps the coaching relationship live between sessions.\n\nFor a student: one place that knows what they have done, what they got wrong, and what to do next.\n\nFor Joshua: one workspace to assign, publish, review, and reply without losing the one-to-one feel that his practice is sold on.\n\nSuccess is a student who logs in without being chased, knows the next action without asking, and can point to what improved.\n\n## Positioning\n\n**A real coach is in it.**\n\nThe claim a competing product cannot truthfully copy is that a named human being is on the other side of the platform, has met this student, and has looked at this student's work. Assignments come from him. Messages are answered by him. The material was authored by him. Nothing in the product should be built in a way that would still be true if the coach were removed, and nothing should imply a coach is present where one is not.\n\n## Operating Context\n\nThe platform sits inside a coaching practice that runs partly outside it. Future work must not assume the app is the whole system.\n\n- Coaching sessions happen on video and are transcribed. Session records, notes, and recaps originate outside the app.\n- Booking and payment run through their own flow (packages, booking passes, a public booking microsite, single sessions).\n- Students also practice on third-party platforms. Work done elsewhere is real work, and the app is not the only record of a student's effort.\n- Joshua authors material ahead of time and assigns it, rather than the system generating it on demand.\n- Students use both desktop and mobile, in focused study blocks and in short check-ins.\n\n## Capabilities and Constraints\n\nConfirmed surfaces: authentication and onboarding, the foyer, the classroom, guided drills and the question bank, full-passage Reading Comprehension, the Activity Centre, the Testing Centre and practice test runs, the wrong answer journal, saved sets, analytics and history, scheduling and booking, the inbox, the boot...

answers, assignments.
- A real named instructor with a real coaching practice behind the product.

Absent, and never to be fabricated:

- No testimonials, case studies, press coverage, score-improvement statistics, or named customer references exist in this repo. Do not write them into any surface.
- No pricing, licensing, or availability claims are established here.
- No third-party or copyright framing anywhere in student-facing copy.

## Product Principles

1. **Truthfulness over performance.** Every number, badge, streak, status, and progress claim traces to real stored data. An honest empty state beats invented content. If nothing has been assigned, the product says so and stops. This is the one principle carried forward unchanged from the previous record, and it is the hardest of the five.

2. **Compute the next step; do not present a grid.** A student arriving at a surface should be told what to do, not shown a menu and left to choose. A card grid is what a product offers when it does not know the student.

3. **Attempt before explanation.** The student commits to an answer before the product explains anything. Passive reading does not build the skill. Reveal copy addresses what the student actually chose, not a generic key.

4. **A measurement is never a gate.** Drills and checks measure after teaching. A miss is framed as the next thing to work on, not a grade or a failure. Nothing in the product rewards attendance rather than work.

5. **The coach is not simulated.** Where the product speaks for Joshua, it is because Joshua said it. Where he has not, the product does not fill the silence with something that sounds like him. This is the operational form of the positioning claim.

## Accessibility & Inclusion

**WCAG 2.1 AA is a binding floor across every surface.** It is a defect gate, not an aspiration. A finding below the AA bar is a bug and is fixed, not argued.

- Text contrast is at least 4.5:1 against its actual rendered background, and at least 3:1 for large text and for the visual boundaries of interactive controls. Students read dense argument text on this platform for hours at a time.
- Every interactive element has a visible focus indicator.
- `prefers-reduced-motion` is respected. Motion is never load-bearing for understanding or for completing a task.
- Touch targets are 44x44px, and meet it by being that size rather than by a side effect such as wrapped text. This one is a house standard above the floor, not part of it: 44x44 is WCAG 2.5.5, which is Level AAA. The binding target-size rule is 2.5.8 at 24x24, so a control that clears 24x24 on a fine pointer clears the floor and is not a defect.
- A dense row of marks that exists to be read at a glance is the one place the 44x44 house standard yields. The Report Card's ledger is 22 to 26 ticks inside an 820px card; at 44px wide they would need 968 to 1144px, so meeting the standard means scrolling or wrapping, and the ledger stops being a single picture of the run. They are 44 tall and 28 to 30 wide, they clear 2.5.8, and that is the settled answer rather than an open defect. Joshua's call, 2026-09-09.
- Where a control has to stay small because it sits in chrome set in 11 or 13px type, the 44x44 target may be an explicit transparent overlay centred on the control instead of the control's own box. An overlay is a target somebody designed and it is proved by hit-testing the four edges; wrapped text is an accident and still does not count. The runner's rail controls, the door's crumb out and the demonstration's Play button are all built this way.
- Screen readers are informed of state that changes without a page load: unread counts, send confirmations, and live updates.
- Contrast is verified against what actually renders in the browser, not against the token values a theme is assumed to apply. The theme layer has shipped a light card on a near-black page whil...

=================== polish.md
> **Additional context needed**: quality bar (MVP vs flagship).

Perform a meticulous final pass to catch all the small details that separate good work from great work. The difference between shipped and polished.

## Design System Discovery

Aligning the feature to the design system is **not optional**. Polish without alignment is decoration on top of drift, and it makes the next person's job harder. Discovery comes before any other polish work.

1. **Find the design system**: Search for design system documentation, component libraries, style guides, or token definitions. Study the core patterns: design principles, target audience, color tokens, spacing scale, typography styles, component API, motion conventions.
2. **Note the conventions**: How are shared components imported? What spacing scale is used? Which colors come from tokens vs hard-coded values? What motion and interaction patterns are established? What flow shapes are used for comparable actions (modal vs full-page, inline vs route, save-on-blur vs explicit submit)?
3. **Identify drift, then name the root cause**: For every deviation, classify it as a **missing token** (the value should exist in the system but doesn't), a **one-off implementation** (a shared component already exists but wasn't used), or a **conceptual misalignment** (the feature's flow, IA, or hierarchy doesn't match neighboring features). The fix differs by category — patch the value, swap to the shared component, or rework the flow. Fixing the symptom without naming the cause is how drift compounds.

If a design system exists, polish **must** align the feature with it. If none exists, polish against the conventions visible in the codebase. **If anything about the system is ambiguous, ask — never guess at design system principles.**

## Pre-Polish Assessment

Understand the current state and goals before touching anything:

1. **Review completeness**:
   - Is it functionally complete?
   - Are there known issues to preserve (mark with TODOs)?
   - What's the quality bar? (MVP vs flagship feature?)
   - When does it ship? (How much time for polish?)

2. **Think experience-first**: Who actually uses this, and what's the best possible experience for them? Effective design beats decorative polish — a feature that looks beautiful but fights the user's flow is not polished. Walk the path from their perspective before opening DevTools.

3. **Identify polish areas**:
   - Visual inconsistencies
   - Spacing and alignment issues
   - Interaction state gaps
   - Copy inconsistencies
   - Edge cases and error states
   - Loading and transition smoothness
   - Information architecture and flow drift (does this feature reveal complexity the way neighboring features do?)

4. **Triage cosmetic vs functional**: Classify each issue as **cosmetic** (looks off, doesn't impede the user) or **functional** (breaks, blocks, or confuses the experience). When polish time is tight, functional issues ship first; cosmetic ones can land in a follow-up. Quality should be consistent — never perfect one corner while leaving another rough.

**CRITICAL**: Polish is the last step, not the first. Don't polish work that's not functionally complete.

## Polish Systematically

Work through these dimensions methodically:

### Visual Alignment & Spacing

- **Pixel-perfect alignment**: Everything lines up to grid
- **Consistent spacing**: All gaps use spacing scale (no random 13px gaps)
- **Optical alignment**: Adjust for visual weight (icons may need offset for optical centering)
- **Responsive consistency**: Spacing and alignment work at all breakpoints
- **Grid adherence**: Elements snap to baseline grid

**Check**:
- Enable grid overlay and verify alignment
- Check spacing with browser inspector
- Test at multiple viewport sizes
- Look for elements that "feel" off

### Information Architecture & Flow

Visual polish on a misshapen flow is wasted...

=================== audit.md
Run systematic **technical** quality checks and generate a comprehensive report. Don't fix issues — document them for other commands to address.

This is a code-level audit, not a design critique. Check what's measurable and verifiable in the implementation.

## Diagnostic Scan

Run comprehensive checks across 5 dimensions. Score each dimension 0-4 using the criteria below.

### 1. Accessibility (A11y)

**Check for**:
- **Contrast issues**: Text contrast ratios < 4.5:1 (or 7:1 for AAA)
- **Missing ARIA**: Interactive elements without proper roles, labels, or states
- **Keyboard navigation**: Missing focus indicators, illogical tab order, keyboard traps
- **Semantic HTML**: Improper heading hierarchy, missing landmarks, divs instead of buttons
- **Alt text**: Missing or poor image descriptions
- **Form issues**: Inputs without labels, poor error messaging, missing required indicators

**Score 0-4**: 0=Inaccessible (fails WCAG A), 1=Major gaps (few ARIA labels, no keyboard nav), 2=Partial (some a11y effort, significant gaps), 3=Good (WCAG AA mostly met, minor gaps), 4=Excellent (WCAG AA fully met, approaches AAA)

### 2. Performance

**Check for**:
- **Layout thrashing**: Reading/writing layout properties in loops
- **Expensive animations**: Casual layout-property animation, unbounded blur/filter/shadow effects, or effects that visibly drop frames
- **Missing optimization**: Images without lazy loading, unoptimized assets, missing will-change
- **Bundle size**: Unnecessary imports, unused dependencies
- **Render performance**: Unnecessary re-renders, missing memoization

**Score 0-4**: 0=Severe issues (layout thrash, unoptimized everything), 1=Major problems (no lazy loading, expensive animations), 2=Partial (some optimization, gaps remain), 3=Good (mostly optimized, minor improvements possible), 4=Excellent (fast, lean, well-optimized)

### 3. Theming

**Check for**:
- **Hard-coded colors**: Colors not using design tokens
- **Broken dark mode**: Missing dark mode variants, poor contrast in dark theme
- **Inconsistent tokens**: Using wrong tokens, mixing token types
- **Theme switching issues**: Values that don't update on theme change

**Score 0-4**: 0=No theming (hard-coded everything), 1=Minimal tokens (mostly hard-coded), 2=Partial (tokens exist but inconsistently used), 3=Good (tokens used, minor hard-coded values), 4=Excellent (full token system, dark mode works perfectly)

### 4. Responsive Design

**Check for**:
- **Fixed widths**: Hard-coded widths that break on mobile
- **Touch targets**: Interactive elements < 44x44px
- **Horizontal scroll**: Content overflow on narrow viewports
- **Text scaling**: Layouts that break when text size increases
- **Missing breakpoints**: No mobile/tablet variants

**Score 0-4**: 0=Desktop-only (breaks on mobile), 1=Major issues (some breakpoints, many failures), 2=Partial (works on mobile, rough edges), 3=Good (responsive, minor touch target or overflow issues), 4=Excellent (fluid, all viewports, proper touch targets)

### 5. Anti-Patterns (CRITICAL)

Check against ALL the **DON'T** guidelines from the parent impeccable skill (already loaded in this context). Look for AI slop tells (AI color palette, gradient text, glassmorphism, hero metrics, card grids, generic fonts) and general design anti-patterns (gray on color, nested cards, bounce easing, redundant copy).

**Score 0-4**: 0=AI slop gallery (5+ tells), 1=Heavy AI aesthetic (3-4 tells), 2=Some tells (1-2 noticeable), 3=Mostly clean (subtle issues only), 4=No AI tells (distinctive, intentional design)

## Generate Report

### Audit Health Score

| # | Dimension | Score | Key Finding |
|---|-----------|-------|-------------|
| 1 | Accessibility | ? | [most critical a11y issue or "--"] |
| 2 | Performance | ? | |
| 3 | Responsive Design | ? | |
| 4 | Theming | ? | |

=== critique table
### Design health score (journey)

| # | Heuristic | Score | Key issue |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Visibility of system status | 3 | Runner exemplary. The marking screen polls silently, then "Still being marked" with no time and no path. |
| 2 | Match system / real world | 2 | "Read" means step 1 on the door, step 4 on the ready beat, "found the move" on the card. "The move" is never defined before the card. |
| 3 | User control and freedom | 2 | Pause exists and works; every pre-run surface says it does not. No resume after refresh. Done is the only action while marking. |
| 4 | Consistency and standards | 1 | Three definitions of Colour, two names for step 4, silent vs out-loud reading, "No pause button" vs a Pause button, "91% carried" on the door vs no arithmetic on the card, three type systems, four labels for Start. |
| 5 | Error prevention | 3 | Leave confirm, mic gate, dead-input detection, phone warning. Gap: the walkthrough coaches the faults the judge penalises. |
| 6 | Recognition rather than recall | 3 | The card asks her to recognise "a Read", "the move", "coloured", which the door never taught under those names. |
| 7 | Flexibility and efficiency | 2 | The section page re-scrolls the whole method on run four; 3 s beat cannot be skipped; 22 tab stops on the card. |
| 8 | Aesthetic and minimalist | 3 | Runner near perfect. Chip restates the H1; counts row restates the drawer; card console is a stat block. |
| 9 | Error recovery | 2 | Upload and mic copy is excellent. A slow or refused grade has no recovery path; that is the shape of the 8-hour incident. |
| 10 | Help and documentation | 3 | Plenty of help; it contradicts itself. |
| **Total** | | **24/40** | **Acceptable** |

Design review and detector agree on: the chip restating the H1, the two card grids, the side stripe on the card, the hero-metric console, the hover lifts, sub-11px labels, and the three em dashes in unrendered branches. === CONTEXT vocabulary run/Attempt
28:Reserved term. The existing question-bank engine: a timed run over multiple-choice questions pulled by `qid`. An Activity is NOT a Drill.
99:Delivering a stimulus as a flat run of facts rather than a story in her own words. The opposite failure to Reciting: Coverage looks perfect and the argument was never held together.
110:**Attempt**:
111:One complete run of one section by one student. A section may be attempted any number of times, and every Attempt keeps its own date, its own recording and its own Report Card. Nothing is overwritten and nothing is hidden once done.
112:_Avoid_: Try, retry, session (reserved for a tutoring session), run
124:The page a student meets before a section run, and the parts of it that carry
129:The page a student lands on before running an RRC section: the method as four Beats, her last few Attempts, and the one control that starts the run. `SectionLaunch` in code, `.sl-` in CSS. It replaces the editorial intro for section mode only; the eight typed Activities keep theirs.
137:One mark in the row that draws a past Attempt: one per stimulus slot, placed at its authored `item_order` rather than its array position. Four states, because three would lie — `recorded`, `notJudged` (the judge never answered, so the mark is hollow), `silent` (she spoke nothing into that stimulus), `missing`.
141:The clock the section was meant to fit, taken from the assignment's time goal at her resolved accommodation. It measures elapsed time and nothing else, and a run is only ever compared against a Target equal to its own.

description: Tighten the measure to 60ch and commit the polish pass
command: cd /c/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/lsatu; sed -i 's|/\* The measure held the card.s column and not the prose inside the open|/* The measure held the card'"'"'s column and not the prose inside the open|' src/features/activity-centre/components/report-card.css; node - <<'EOF'
const fs=require("fs");const p="src/features/activity-centre/components/report-card.css";let s=fs.readFileSync(p,"utf8");
const a=".rc__movement p,\n.rc__movement li,\n.rc__movement blockquote {\n  max-width: 72ch;\n}";
if(!s.includes(a)){console.error("anchor");process.exit(1)}
s=s.replace(a,".rc__movement p,\n.rc__movement li,\n.rc__movement blockquote {\n  /* 60ch, not 72: Lexend's zero is wide (8.5px at 14px), so 72ch is 612px and\n     about 80 real characters. 60ch measures 510px, 66 characters. */\n  max-width: 60ch;\n}");
fs.writeFileSync(p,s);console.log("ok");
EOF
git add src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionProgress.tsx src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionProgress.test.tsx src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionReportCard.tsx src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionReport.tsx src/features/activity-centre/components/report-card.css src/features/activity-centre/components/section-launch.css src/dev/reportCardPreview.tsx && git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'
RRC: the polish pass

Three em dashes out of student copy. Attempt where the door said run. The
Recall verdict in words rather than as an enum. The card's own primary
control in place of the corridor's Button, whose emerald the card's brief
vetoes. Status regions mounted from the start so their change is what gets
announced. Prose inside the open stimulus capped at a real measure. One dead
rule and three duplicated selectors gone from the door.

Step 12 of the RRC drill fix plan, part one.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Fable 5.1 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF
git log --oneline -1

## assistant

Step 12 is done and up as [PR #111](https://github.com/vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db/pull/111), an integration branch carrying Steps 8 through 12. Merging it closes the four smaller PRs by content. Both score targets were missed, and the plan file says why.

**Scores, measured on the merged tree.**

| | Before | After | Target |
|---|---|---|---|
| Audit | 13/20 | 15/20 | 17 |
| Critique | 24/40 | 26/40 | 32 |

**What the polish changed.** Em dashes are out of every rendered student sentence. The record is called an Attempt and the figure is called held, from the library row to the wait screen. The Recall verdict reads as words instead of an enum. The card's Done, Check again and Run these again buttons wear the card's own control instead of the shell's emerald Button. Status regions are mounted from the start so their change is announced. Prose in the open stimulus is capped at a real measure. The closing line no longer hardcodes twenty-five minutes or says "No going back" under "You can pause".

**Two defects the re-run found in this session's own work, both fixed.** Steps 9 and 10 fought once merged: the scoped meadow rule outranked the unscoped phone rule and the phone hero collapsed to 456px. And the beat harness had no hidden-heading rule, so Assessment A measured a rail the product never ships.

**Detector config, corrected.** The Report Card's waiver did not cover its TSX, and the door's ignore-file entry hid 69 sanctioned-world notes. Both worlds now carry reasoned per-file waivers added through the CLI's own command. The final scan over six files returns one finding, the playhead stripe, left unwaived on purpose.

**Why the targets were missed.** What remains is shape, not polish: the door teaches the method six times and runs 4,400px, the console gives the verdict three ways in its first 80px, and the beat's rail and footer say two true things that contradict. Each is a shape conversation rather than a fix. The plan lists five recommended next actions.

**One question for you.** When the six-second microphone check expires unheard, the beat now counts her in anyway, exactly as Step 8's spec asked. Assessment A argues that is the run the whole check exists to prevent, and that it should hold with a "Start anyway" button instead. I left it as specified. Your call.

Suite is 106 files, 2790 tests, `tsc` clean. Working tree is back on main. The vault wrapup is still outstanding.
